The Codex doesn't pause for grief. Hudson blinks — and it's already there, overlaying everything, syncing. No pen. No paper. The mantle just... transfers.

◈ THE TABLE — it is not a table. It's a ring of pale light, and around it, things with too many angles to be faces. Above it all — the audience. Trillions of eyes, and every one of them knows your name. The System speaks, and for once it isn't performing: "HUMANITY PRESENTS ITS CASE."
◈ THE AUDIT — the System reads your run back to you, and the galaxy listens. A lesser seat — global standing, waves 1–3 faced (13/11/5), with 4 wave-4 fought, 10 contests survived, 3 crises weathered. The table does not rise. It prices. Ladder — National Standing reached (Fight 3 · Forage 10 · Craft 5 · Talk 20 · Explore 38 → 24 · capped)
◈ For a full seat, the ledger still wants: only 0 of 2 different wave-5 monsters faced; no capstone deed delivered (military tribute, trade charter, or feast embassy).
◈ THE WAR PATH — The System pauses — actually pausing. It keeps a list of every tooth pulled, and for once it reads the list instead of the press release. On day 78, Baraka put down The Loud Beast.

◈ THE BELOVED — The audience is chanting. You don't know the words — it isn't a language with words — but you know the feeling. The table can't move against the cast's favorite. That's leverage. Tonight, the cast is your village.
The table has read your ledger. The protected outnumber the killed; the food truth was shared. The light road is open.
Jae tells the hunt — the real one, with the fear left in. The audience goes silent in the good way. Silence, at this scale, is deafening.
The feast song — the one about eating like it matters, because it does. Trillions of beings who never ate anything hear it and feel hungry for the first time. The ratings spike is audible.
You spend it all — the love, the light, the finale slot — on people the cameras never found. You name the unfilmed villages. You name the dead. The audience weeps in eleven dimensions. This is what the show was for, it turns out.
◈ THE BELOVED — the last choice is yours, and it's live.
◈ THE BELOVED — "Let the audience speak."
The show becomes the government. Strange. It works. Trillions of viewers hold the table to account in real time, and humanity's seat is wherever the cameras are. You made popularity into a constitution — and constitutions, unlike ratings, don't dip.
You made a meal large enough for another place. You learned the names of the people who kept finding one.
You kept the welcome larger than the price of a plate.
◈ Amina Diallo walks out of the light alone, which is how she walked in — and the audience, which came to be entertained, leaves humming the village's name. The village endures, loved and unbeholden, and the lights stay warm behind her.
◈ THE OVERSIGHT —
You won. Say it plainly: you won. The village endures. The table signed. The galaxy applauds, and the applause is real, and you earned it.
And somewhere out there — past the cameras, past the light — in rows of humming glass, the little ones are still napping.
The mourners asked you, and you changed the subject.
The System put a pod on the screen, and you let the segment play.
They are still there. Still small. Still waiting for someone to decide they matter more than the moment.
That is what the word means. Not the watching — anyone can watch. The oversight is the looking away. The most terrible thing the System ever did, it did by accident, because it was bored. You did it on purpose, because you were busy.
The village will tell your story for years. It will be a good story. Listen for the part they don't tell.
💛 You hold Notebook. Route optimizations, glitch lists, apocalypse patch notes. 'DO NOT TRUST THE CACHE BY THE RIVER.' Your magnum opus, half-finished. Your gifts are not mastered — but they are lived-in, and they are with you. The feast was the weapon.
